metadata polynomials first, including the balanced partition and
partner relocation. Choose the digit width
\(b=\lceil\log_2(B+2)\rceil^D\) with fixed sufficiently large
\(D\). Then \(W=\Theta(b)\) pays those polynomials and
\(L=\Theta(\log B)\). Prime selection, the small dyadic axis,
coarse CRT and padding have their original polynomial or
linear-volume secondary-logarithm charges.

The actual square local Fourier words still use their original
integer lookups, diagonal order and completed matchings. All prime
axes and CRT merges remain synchronized. Every numerical matching
is evaluated exactly modulo its original chosen odd modulus,
followed by its single target-based recovery. Native dummy
coordinates are cropped only after their completed restoring
boundary. Scalar tables and roots are still prepared once; global
diagonals use sequential prefix products in \(O(M)\) wide word
products per slot. Those products use the independent classical
Sch\"onhage--Strassen algorithm, whose word logarithms are
\(O(\log L)\). No call uses this new multiplier as its backend.

Accordingly two forward transforms, the pointwise product and the
inverse have total cost \(O(BL^{\tau_L}\operatorname{polylog}L)\)
plus once-only polynomial setup. The old polynomial guard still
makes the convolution error below \(1/4\); exact rounding and
the linear \(O(B)\) carry scan return the ordinary binary product.
All tapes and private pads are cleared as in the earlier composition.
Finally \(12387/10^8<a_L\) absorbs the fixed secondary logarithms.
In fact \(a_L/(1+a_L)<12387/10^8\), so this plain bound exceeds
the old unequal-field leading saving, rather than merely retuning
its former \(123/10^6\) display.
\end{proof}

The bounded \texttt{balanced\_native\_blocks\_probe.cpp} checks
integer block partitions and explicit equal-piece/residual-bit
schedules, complete padded native rectangles, and dirty-helper
endpoint words with transported partner indices and inverses.
Its small native tuples check all quotient labels, including
unequal block counts. Negative controls reject an omitted final
mask, nonzero \(FG\), untranslated partners and premature removal
of an even-native dummy coordinate. Metadata integers and symbolic
bit layouts have at most 1024 bits or labels; native tuple grids
have at most 2048 points. The self-only 64 MiB/10 CPU-second guard
remains mandatory. These are structural toy checks, not an execution
of the large-radix machine or proof that tiny toy radices satisfy
its asymptotic cube lower bound. The general proof supplies that
shape and all-size cost argument. Balanced grouping and the dirty
four-mask identity use elementary techniques and the folder's
existing frozen-mask construction; global novelty is not asserted.

The earlier recovery witness executes the full five-native-kernel word on
\(4\times6\times8\) Gaussian arrays before balanced recovery.
Its 6149 guarded assertions check 3072 output positions in 16 fixtures,
including an odd modulus divisible by the native prime five.
All 16 prematurely rounded controls and 12 undersized-modulus controls
fail. It checks the unchanged completed matching/recovery interface
and the earlier rational exponent gap, rather than executing the
new physical routing word or the growing integer multiplier.

At the preceding comparison checkpoint, the public repository's master
was pinned at \texttt{29c704f0542b}. Its README was re-read for this
refinement and still reports unconditional integer saving
\(4222422970/2^{46}\). The selected community certificate and
leaderboard were also re-read and still report the stronger saving
\(0.003125804\) with their stated conditional framework. Our new
plain saving \(12387/10^8\) exceeds the former unconditional
README numeral, but the complete transfer is internally reviewed
without a new independent proof-check receipt or exhaustive novelty
audit. These comparisons and finite checks do not establish an
accepted record or complete the active stronger public-record objective.

\begin{corollary}[Exact dyadic complex kernels and charged rounding]
\label{cor:dyadicphase}
Let \(k\ge0\), \(N=2^k\), \(B\ge1\), and \(q\ge0\).
Suppose each of \(\ell\ge1\) lanes has complex inputs
\(x_j=2^{-q}(a_j+i b_j)\), where the signed integers satisfy
\(|a_j|,|b_j|<2^B\). There is a fixed finite-alphabet, fixed-tape
machine returning the exact values of \(C^{\otimes k}x\) as
Gaussian integer numerators with common denominator \(2^{q+k}\), in
\[
 O\bigl(N\ell(B+k+1)(k+1)^{\gamma_{\rm mix}}
       +\log(q+2)\bigr)
\]
bit steps. The same holds for the inverse phase tensor. Alternatively,
the machine returns dyadic values on the original \(2^{-q}\) grid,
with error at most \(2^{-q-1}\) in each real and imaginary component,
within the same bound. The exponent \(q+k\) is stored as a binary
descriptor; a string of \(q+k\) zero bits is not materialized.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Put \(h=B+2k+4\) and \(P=2^h+1\). Its residue width is
\(s=h+1=O(B+k+1)\). Construct its binary description and convert
the signed input numerators to canonical residues in \(O(N\ell s)\)
steps, retaining their ordinary address order. Apply
Theorem~\ref{thm:mixedphase} in \(A_P\). No complex scalar is
approximated during that invocation.

The matrix \(2C\) has Gaussian integer entries \(1+i,1-i\).
Consequently
\[
 z=2^k C^{\otimes k}(a+i b)\in\Z[i]^{N}
\]
in each lane. Unitarity of \(C^{\otimes k}\) gives
\[
 |\Re z_j|,|\Im z_j|
 \le |z_j|<2^{B+(3k+1)/2}<\frac P2.
\]
Multiply each returned residue by \(2^k\) modulo \(P\), using
Lemma~\ref{lem:shortshift}. This costs
\(O(N\ell s\log^4(k+2))\), including scalar preparation.
Since \(2^k\) clears the target denominator in
\(\Z[i,1/2]\), reduction of \(z\) is exactly the resulting
residue. For each component, subtract \(P\) if its canonical
residue exceeds \((P-1)/2\). The displayed bound proves that this
linear-volume operation recovers the unique actual integer component,
not merely a congruent answer. The returned exact value is
\(z_j/2^{q+k}\).

For the grid-rounded alternative, divide each signed component of
\(z_j\) by \(2^k\), rounding to nearest with a fixed tie rule.
Scanning its binary magnitude once gives the quotient, rounding bit
and carry in \(O(s)\) steps. The resulting component error is at
most \(2^{-q-1}\). Only the final return is rounded; there is no
accumulation of unpriced internal rounding. Adding \(k\) to the
common exponent descriptor costs \(O(\log(q+2)+\log(k+2))\).
The inverse is \(X^{\otimes k}C^{\otimes k}\); reversing the
complete address blocks costs linear volume and has the same norm and
denominator bounds. Finally
\(\log^4(k+2)=O((k+1)^{\gamma_{\rm mix}})\), so all these costs
fit the asserted running time. Setup, signed conversion, residue
components, exact reconstruction and the external output copy are
included on a fixed number of tapes.
\end{proof}

This supplies a bit-level exact dyadic implementation of the phase
kernel used by the complex Fourier reduction. In particular, for
\(B\ge k+1\) its width overhead is a fixed factor. The equality
\(\|C^{\otimes k}u\|_2=\|u\|_2\) also bounds propagation of
input quantization error through this particular kernel. A complete
integer-multiplication transfer must still price the outer roots,
scalar operations, native-layout movement and their total error; this
corollary does not identify those costs with the RAM work bound.

The bounded \texttt{dyadic\_phase\_recovery\_probe.cpp} compares a
modular butterfly with the independent dense Gaussian-integer formula
for \((2C)^{\otimes k}\), including independently conjugated inverse
coefficients. Its 99597 assertions cover 882 fixtures, 16002 output
positions, \(k\le6\), \(B\le7\), and at most 64 addresses.
It rejects 14010 omitted-\(2^k\) recoveries, 13321 truncated modular
halves and 28 undersized-modulus fixtures; negative outputs and
composite moduli occur. It checks the recovery and rounding identities,
not the asymptotic compiler, under the self-only resource limits.

\paragraph{What the same budget says about the RAM record attempt.}
The compiler's operation-count induction uses the primitive exponent
only to require that it be smaller than \(z\). In the public RAM,
Lemma~\ref{lem:rampoint} and the complete affine/quadratic tables price
word adapters in linear array volume; a rectangular transpose is an
explicit charged array construction using division and remainder on
indices. Thus their primitive exponent is zero. The shrinking-exit
lemma permits every \(z>t=0.99688\), rather than imposing the much
stronger one-step ratio condition used for the easy bit-side check.
Copying a stack of array addresses and shape descriptors, rather than
assuming a free mutable stack, contributes a fixed polynomial in
\(\log(k+2)\) per call; local field descriptions have polynomial
\(k\)-size and the same complete-vector amortization as above.
There is room to absorb this factor below the kernel exponent
\(0.99689\). This independently checks its analytic budget. The
following construction also supplies the actual generic theorem
arguments and uniform syntactic program witnesses; the arithmetic
budget alone would not supply those witnesses.

Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:directbridge} to the explicit strictified engine.
Use one target type per actual live role, each with multiplicity one;
use the ranks of its actual paid letters in chronological order.
The strict moment certificate is already the required unnormalized
\(\mathrm{RateQ}(t,1)\) inequality. This supplies every rule and
bridge field without a grouped histogram factory, an equality with
the vendor's powered word, or a profile-specific engine theorem.
The public \texttt{BU.tensor\_engine} in \texttt{TensorEng/EngOf.lean}
is a related packaged route to the same generic engine theorem; the
present argument uses the latter directly, with attribution to its
mixed-target compiler.

Choose
\[
 \beta=1-\frac{311}{100000}=0.99689,\qquad
 \nu=1-\frac{31}{10000}=0.9969.
\]
Then \(0<t<\beta<\nu<1\). Applying
\texttt{Eng.engine\_seam\_final} with \(n_0=M\) gives the closed
\texttt{Can} kernel guarantee at envelope
\(E_\beta(k)=\lceil(k+1)^\beta\rceil\), for all four scalar-dependency
colours \texttt{scalar}, \texttt{left}, \texttt{right}, \texttt{both},
and both allowed multiplication modes. In particular the
\texttt{scalar} case covers transforms of prepared chirp arrays.
The definition of \texttt{Can}
includes one fixed finite program, a fixed work constant, and a bound
\((2^k+2)^d\) on its integer words. To get uniformity simultaneously
in dimension and data, instantiate its index family with all pairs
\((k,x)\), \(x\in\mathbb C^{2^k}\); a singleton index per input
would not prove the same statement. Its output is the canonical
\texttt{ripple} array, with all class matching, remainders, preparation
and output work included by the compiler.

Lemma~\ref{lem:paramtransfer} applies. The all-length Fourier construction consumes this guarantee at exactly
one algorithmic call site, \texttt{Bench.gleamT}, plus
\(1\le E_\beta(k)\le k+1\), monotonicity, and
\(E_\beta(k)\le2(k+1)^\beta\). All hold for \(0<\beta<1\).
A source scan of the dependency directory finds no other call to
\texttt{hills\_program}; outside its defining modules, exponent uses
occur only in this envelope interface and the final asymptotic analysis.
Thus the charged working-transform and arbitrary-length proofs admit
this replacement, with their existing deterministic layout and root
selection algorithms.

More explicitly, their order program returns
\[
 D(n)=\begin{cases}
 (2n)\,\mathrm{barge}(n).\mathrm{vol}\,2^{\mathrm{orderE}(n)},
   &2^{\mathrm{orderE}(n)}\le n,\\
 n,&\text{otherwise},
 \end{cases}
 \qquad 0<D(n)<8n^3.
\]
In the first branch \(4\mid D(n)\). The prepared scalar
\(i=\zeta^{D(n)/4}\), the chirp root of order \(2n\), and the
working roots are obtained from the supplied canonical
\(\zeta=\operatorname{root}(D(n))\) by charged scalar powering.
The direct branch needs no supplied \(i\); it has polynomial cost
and is eventually unused. The chirp factor paired with the input is
prepared data, so the DFT uses \texttt{Prog false}; no product of
two input-dependent data values is introduced.

The existing \texttt{Ration}/\texttt{storehouse} extraction then gives
one order program, one solve program and a work bound for every positive
\(n\), with all integer values, addresses, allocation and work bounded
by a fixed power of \(n+2\). The asymptotic part is
\[
 O\!\left(n(\log n)^\beta(\log\log n)^2
       +(\log n+2)^d\right)
   =O\!\left(n(\log n)^\nu\right).
\]
The fixed \(d\) and all enormous template constants are included.
The two strict gaps are \(\beta-t=\nu-\beta=10^{-5}\).
The \texttt{DFTProgram} order limit \(1024n^3\) follows from the
stronger \(8n^3\) bound above. The following theorem records the
complete mathematical composition. Its internal review is separate
from checking a new Lean instance or obtaining public acceptance.

\begin{theorem}[All-length exact DFT in the charged complex RAM]
\label{thm:ramdft}
There are fixed finite programs \texttt{order}, \texttt{solve} and a
natural-valued allowance \(W\) satisfying the public
\texttt{DFTProgram(order,solve,W)} definition, with
\[
 W(n)=O\bigl(n(\log n)^{9969/10000}\bigr).
\]
For every positive \(n\), the order program selects the canonical
supplied root with order \(0<D(n)<8n^3\). The solve program computes
the exact positive-sign DFT on every complex input vector, in
multiplication mode \texttt{false}. All preparation and allocation are
charged, and integer values, work and arena addresses are bounded by
one fixed power of \(n+2\).
This is an internal mathematical consequence of the explicit finite
certificates and cited compiler theorems, not a claim of a newly
Lean-checked instance or an externally accepted record.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Here are the witnesses and quantifiers underlying the preceding
calculation. Use the actual raw word of
Proposition~\ref{prop:recordunit}, form its explicit 1024-fold product
with the point \texttt{CLEAR} steps of Lemma~\ref{lem:wordproduct},
complete targets to \(17^{1024}\), and split paid families at
\(16^{1024}\). The resulting finite word, role injection and actual
supplied direction families determine \(E\).
Its fields \texttt{X,R,Q,e,tr,tz,wd} are those data with finite layouts;
\texttt{he,tind,tr\_le,tr\_pos,hP,sep,W\_pos,three,hw} are precisely
the injectivity, independence, rank, separation and all-column facts
proved above. In particular \texttt{hw} concerns arbitrary live inputs
and initially zero scratch. Cleanup is supplied explicitly, rather
than inferred from the weaker imported live-output contract.
The strict rate bound is less than one, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:directbridge} supplies every rule and bridge field.
No equality to the vendor's word implementation is a premise.

For each fixed dependency colour \(a\), instantiate
\texttt{engine\_seam\_final} in mode \texttt{false} on
\[
 \iota=\coprod_{k\in\mathbb N}(\mathrm{Bits}(k)\to\mathbb C),
 \qquad k(k,x)=k,\qquad v(k,x)=x.
\]
Eliminating the existential in \texttt{Can} yields one finite closed
kernel code \(K_a\), with fixed work and word-bound constants, for
all these inputs simultaneously. Reindexing into a sector's dimension
and data leaves this same code unchanged: this follows directly from
\texttt{Able.reindex}, which reuses its code witness. There are only
four colours, so all their constants have a common fixed upper bound.
The word is fixed before the input length varies; its enormous finite
description is not advice depending on \(n\).

Use these four code witnesses in the unique kernel call site
\texttt{Bench.gleamT}. All other occurrences of \texttt{hills} in
the downstream construction are work bounds or the envelope properties
listed in Lemma~\ref{lem:paramtransfer}. In particular the apparent
per-beat use in \texttt{repeatM} is a work allowance: its underlying
\texttt{Able.loop} constructs \texttt{Code.loop l q f}, whose loop
length comes from the integer program \texttt{l}, not from that
allowance. Weakening a work bound likewise reuses the existing code.
Thus this substitution executes no real-power or ceiling instruction.
Prepared rational and fourth-root coefficients use only allowed scalar
operations; the word's point matrices never multiply two input values.

Lemma~\ref{lem:paramtransfer}, now with these actual uniform witnesses,
constructs \texttt{order} and \texttt{solve} on the universal family of
all positive lengths and all input vectors. In its working branch,
\(4\mid D(n)\), and the supplied canonical root gives
\(i=\zeta^{D(n)/4}\) by the charged \texttt{extractP} program.
The same program supplies the chirp and working roots. The direct
branch uses its supplied root of order \(n\) and needs no \(i\).
Only the branch that is actually executed contributes validity
conditions. Prepared chirp transforms use colour \texttt{scalar};
scaling the data preserves mode \texttt{false}.

With \(E(k)=\lceil(k+1)^\beta\rceil\),
\(\beta=99689/100000\), use the explicit allowance
\[
 W(n)=c_o(\mathrm{fair}_E(d_o,n)+1)+1+
       c_s(\mathrm{fair}_E(d_s,n)+1).
\]
The two positive constants and polynomial degrees come from the fixed
program witnesses, independently of \(n\) and the data. The middle
unit pays for the supplied-root primitive. The parametrized
\texttt{storehouse}/\texttt{flagshipOne} argument proves every
\texttt{DFTProgram} conjunct, including \(W(n)+10(n+2)\) below one
polynomial cap. Fresh array and stack storage creates at most a fixed
number of cells per charged step, so cumulative arena addresses obey
that cap as well. No constant-time whole-array update is introduced.

Finally the bound already derived is
\(O(n(\log n)^\beta(\log\log n)^2+(\log n+2)^d)\), for fixed \(d\).
The direct branch disappears eventually. Since
\(9969/10000-\beta=10^{-5}>0\), the secondary logarithms and fixed
setup term fit the asserted allowance. The finite base lengths retain
their exact charged runs. This proves the claimed fixed-program
statement for every positive length, not merely a rank-moment estimate
or a separate program for each input.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Conditional direct complex Walsh transfer]
\label{cor:ramwht}
Assume the finite unit and its all-column semantics asserted in
Proposition~\ref{prop:recordunit}, its product, completion and splitting
construction, and the stated strict moment bound and compiler bridge are
valid in the same \texttt{Can}/\texttt{ripple} types as the WHT wrapper
in \cite{Sussman,Leaderboard}. Then for every fixed
\(\beta\) with \(0.99688<\beta<1\), there is a fixed program satisfying
the public \texttt{WHTProgram} contract, with work
\[
 O\bigl(2^k(k+1)^\beta\bigr)
 \quad\text{and}\quad o(2^k k).
\]
In particular \(\beta=99689/100000\) would give logarithmic-exponent
saving \(0.00311\). The input is \((k,\mathrm{Complex.I},x)\) with
\(x\in\mathbb C^{2^k}\) and the distinguished exact imaginary unit;
the output is the unnormalized Walsh--Hadamard transform in the public
statement's natural Sylvester order. The fixed program, charged setup,
scaling, allocation, indexing and polynomial bounds are those of that
exact-complex RAM contract. This is not a finite-ring bit bound.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
For fixed \(\beta\) in the stated interval, the strictified engine has
rate exponent \(t=1-39/12500=0.99688<\beta\). Subject to the displayed
assumptions, Lemma~\ref{lem:directbridge} gives the claimed uniform
kernel \texttt{Can} guarantee at envelope
\(\lceil(k+1)^\beta\rceil\). Instantiate the common index type as
\(\coprod_{k\in\mathbb N}(\mathrm{Bits}(k)\to\mathbb C)\), and select
\texttt{Paint.left} and multiplication mode \texttt{false}. Its input,
output, and work have the exact \texttt{givenBits}, \texttt{ripple} and
\(2^k\) envelope form required by
\texttt{BlockWHT.wht\_main\_of\_engine}. The wrapper constructs one
fixed program and charges the diagonal preparation and both scaling
passes; its natural-order identity is the stated Walsh transform.
Because \(0\le\beta<1\), that theorem supplies both work bounds and
the program contract. The strict inequality \(t<\beta\) is required;
this argument does not attain the endpoint \(\beta=t\).
\end{proof}

This is an explicit conditional composition of the manuscript's
internal claims with the published wrapper, not a new finite network,
an externally validated theorem, or a new Lean-checked instance. In
particular the finite recipe is not yet a typed, kernel-checked
\texttt{CU} object. The claimed raw unit reconstructs the public \#6030
lineage; the later \#6167 construction is not certified by this corollary.

The full composition above has been reviewed internally against the
16 \texttt{Eng} fields, 15 \texttt{Rule} fields, six
\texttt{BridgeHyp} fields, universal input quantifier, dependency
colours, actual root branches, work and integer bounds. The bounded
import audit resolves 153 local source modules and 50 normalized overlay
manifest hashes; only Mathlib imports remain outside that closure.
Its absence of admission tokens is provenance evidence, not a Lean
elaboration of this new word. The theorem's saving \(0.0031\) exceeds
the refreshed public source envelope, but external verification and
public record acceptance remain open. It yields no corresponding
\(0.0031\) finite-tape or integer bit saving.

The new bounded C++ probe checks 6911 exact assertions, including 208
frame invariants on sixteen fixtures, four roles and at most 64 addresses
per role. Controls detect \(q^f\) coefficients, wrong absolute phases,
reversed transitions and omitted dirty erasure. This validates a local
component; it does not verify the full public word or establish a record.

\section{Validation and scope}
The companion machine and finite-label audits reconstruct every named
local interface. The bounded C++ witness installs self-only Windows
64 MiB process-memory and 10 process-CPU-second limits before algebra;
it refuses if either cannot be installed.
It checks exact finite counts, dirty scratch restoration, orthonormal
completion, source/inverse dual masks, address-basis transport and
padded frame telescoping. Negative controls omit phases, confuse primal
and dual masks, crop internal padding and share incompatible exponent
reductions.
These witnesses do not instantiate the fixed machine's enormous role
set or measure asymptotic speed. The internal cross-interface audit
checks the algebra, retained layouts, setup, word arithmetic, recursive
volumes and fixed-tape storage in the saved proof. This research
manuscript is not externally refereed or formally verified, and makes
no practical-speed or publication-priority claim. No numerical Gaussian
resampling or main integer multiplier is imported.

\appendix
\section{Residual table}\label{app:labels}
Tensor every entry below by \(Q_0\), except the final scratch-to-sink
entry, where \(Q_0^\perp\) is in the whole later tensor space.
The triple lines on a side wire are denoted \(T_X,T_Y\).
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{.28\textwidth}p{.48\textwidth}p{.16\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Edge & Residual & Dimension\\ \midrule
X in to 2; Y 0 to 1 & \(B\otimes t^\perp\) & \((a_0-1)(H-1)\)\\
X 2 to 3; Y 4 to 5 & \(P_0\otimes t^\perp\) & \(H-1\)\\
X 3 to 6,6 to 7; Y in to 0,1 to 4 & zero & 0\\
Center source to 1 & \(B\otimes F\) & \((a_0-1)H\)\\
Center 1 to 3,3 to 4,4 to 6 & \(P_0\otimes F\) & \(H\)\\
Side source to 0 & \(B\otimes T_Y\) & \(a_0-1\)\\
Side 0 to 2 & \((B\otimes T_Y^\perp)\op(P_0\otimes T_X)\)
 & \((a_0-1)(H-1)+1\)\\
Side 2 to 5 & \(P_0\otimes(T_X\op T_Y)^\perp\) & \(H-2\)\\
Side 5 to 7 & \(P_0\otimes T_Y\) & 1\\
Scratch last to sink & \((A\otimes F)\otimes Q_0^\perp\) & \(a_0H(f_0-1)\)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\(T_X\subseteq T_Y^\perp\) proves the side 2-to-5 inclusion.
All remaining inclusions follow from \(A=B\op P_0\) and
\(F=\langle t\rangle\op t^\perp\).
Only center 3-to-4 entries decrease. The final data edges and
interstage data edges have equal labels.

\section{Interchange reconstruction}\label{app:swap}
The following is the local form of the source's method \cite{Source},
with its counts specialized and its tape charges exposed.

\subsection{Finite rational frame}
Use ground size \(g=48\), triples joined at intersection one, and
\(v_0=\binom g3\), \(z_0=3\binom{g-3}2\), \(n_0=v_0^3\),
\(I_0=3v_0^2\), \(m_0=g^3\).
Use the eight-step scalar program of Lemma~\ref{lem:scalar} over
\(\F_2\), with the following DIFFERENT incidence maps:
\(G_{j,T}=R_{T,j}=[j\in T]\) for \(j\in[g]\), one side copy per
ordered intersection-one pair, and side coefficient 1. There is no
star coordinate and no halving in this bit variant.
Central intersection parity plus the side term is the identity.
It exchanges data banks and restores arbitrary scratch; its negative
bank sign is the same as a positive sign over \(\F_2\).

For address labels use the DIFFERENT field \(\Q\), with rational form
\(I-J_g/9\), where \(J_g\) is the \(g\)-by-\(g\) all-one matrix.
Its eigenvalues are 1 and
\(1-g/9=-13/3\). Triple lines have norm two and neighbor lines are
orthogonal. The common-label table and residual inclusions above apply.
No orthonormal basis is required here; only rational projection ranks.
There are \(g\) center roles, so
\[
 W_0=2n_0+I_0(v_0z_0+g),\qquad L_0=I_0g^2.
\]
Put \(-P_U\) at X sources, zero at other sources, and positive
projections at gates and sinks. The routed endpoint difference is
\(I\) on every role.
Comparable projection differences have residual rank; the first X
edge is \(2P_U\) and adds one rank per data route. Hence
\[
 s_0=W_0m_0-n_0+2L_0,\qquad
 \frac{W_0}{n_0}=\frac{9633881}{1081},\quad
 \frac{L_0}{n_0}=\frac{432}{1081}.
\]
Thus
\[
 1-\frac{s_0}{W_0m_0}
 =\frac{217}{9633881\cdot110592}.
\]
The exact inequality
\(217\cdot2^{36}>12\cdot9633881\cdot110592\),
and \(\ln m_0<12\), give \(s_0/W_0<m_0^\tau\).

\subsection{Rank pivots and later-field work}
Every fixed rational matrix \(A\) has a factorization
\(A=E_1\Pi E_2\), with invertible lower-triangular \(E_1,E_2\)
and a partial permutation \(\Pi\) with \(\operatorname{rank}A\) ones.
Choose the topmost nonzero row and its rightmost pivot, clear to its
left by lower-triangular column operations and below by lower-triangular
row operations, remove the pivot indices preserving order, and reverse.
Choose one fixed odd prime \(q\) avoiding all denominators and all
nonzero diagonal-unit numerators in the finite frame/factor table.
Finite elimination and trial division construct it once, independently
of every input length.

Write two address chunks as \(m_0\) fields of \(b\) radix-\(q\)
digits. The address permutation
\(\Phi_A(H,D)=(H+AD,D)\pmod {q^b}\) is valid for singular \(A\) too.
Lower-triangular changes are updated in descending coordinate order,
with earlier controls still old, by later-field rotations.
Fixed rational unit scalings cost linear volume: multiplication by a
fixed integer splits the integer interval into a fixed number of
increasing pieces, merged by the unique preimage residue; reversing this
implements its inverse. Negation reverses nonzero blocks and then each
block's bit order. Offset setup is polynomial in \(b\), absorbed by the
complete \(q^b\) fiber. Spectator counters advance amortized.

Apply \(E_1^{-1}\) to \(H\), \(E_2\) to \(D\), the \(\Pi\)
shears, then undo. Each pivot is implemented by
\[
 D_j\gets D_j+H_i;\quad
 \operatorname{SWAP}(H_i,D_j);\quad
 D_j\gets H_i-D_j.
\]
Its outside operations target a later field. Exactly one smaller swap
is used per pivot.

\subsection{Power and arbitrary widths}
Define a separate power-width procedure \(\operatorname{SwapPow}_j\)
on layouts
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 &[B_0][R_{\rm row}][B_{\rm pre}][q^e][B_{\rm gap}][q^e][B_{\rm suf}],\\
 &e=m_0^j,\qquad W_0^j\mid R_{\rm row}.
 \end{aligned}
\]
It exchanges the two selected fields, fixes the designated row and all
spectators, and returns lexicographic order. The pretarget spectator
\(B_{\rm pre}\) is allowed because a pivot need not be the first slot
of its parent chunk. The base \(j=0\) is a fixed-radix digit exchange.
For an internal call, use the already supplied row pool; do not create
new padding or invoke the public arbitrary-width lift.
Split whole rows into equal roles. Common-frame permutations commute with
bitwise XOR, and telescoping gives \(H\gets H+D\) on every row after
bank restoration, by the same logical-input frame invariant used for
the phase construction. Each rank pivot calls
\(\operatorname{SwapPow}_{j-1}\) at volume \(V/W_0\), width
\(e/m_0\), and the inherited designated row pool divided by \(W_0\).
All unselected parent fields are retained as collapsed pretarget, gap
or suffix spectators. Induction supplies exactly \(s_0\) such calls.
Convert that single shear into a whole swap by
\(D\gets D-H;\ H\gets H+D;\ D\gets H-D\).
Only the middle step uses the motif. The normalized recurrence is
\(F(e)\le(s_0/W_0)F(e/m_0)+C\), yielding \(O(e^\tau)\).

For arbitrary \(e\), put \(j=\lceil\log_{m_0}e\rceil\) and choose
the least \(\rho\) with \(q^{2\rho}\ge W_0^j\).
Eventually \(\rho=O(\log e)<e\); the fixed remaining range uses direct
digit exchanges. Exchange the two existing high \(\rho\)-digit fields,
move them together before the low fields, and regard their
\(q^{2\rho}\) values as rows. Pad that range once to a multiple of
\(W_0^j\), by less than two. Partition the existing low width
\(e-\rho\) into consecutive base-\(m_0\) power pieces.
The sum of their costs is \(O(Ve^\tau)\).
Completed calls restore their row coordinates, so crop only after full
role restoration and restore the high-digit layout in \(O(V\log e)\).
Move right-hand short fields left first-to-last, left-hand fields right
last-to-first. No target width is rounded upward.

To swap binary ranges \(2^u\), pad them numerically to the least
\(q^e\ge2^u\), at a volume factor below \(q^2\).
Numerical order is radix lexicographic order; no per-entry conversion
occurs. Apply the whole \(q\)-power swap and crop invalid numerical
coordinates only afterward. The final swap preserves the valid square;
intermediate piece swaps need not. Since \(e=O(u)\), the bound is
\(O(V(u+1)^\tau)\).

\subsection{Fixed tapes and descriptors}
At every power-width child the complete original chunk ranges remain
selected or spectator fields. Only independent role rows shrink.
Park current inactive siblings, copy only the active child, and pop
siblings on return using stack-top heads. The finite \(s_0,W_0\)
affect additive constants, not a multiplier on child time. Reuse the
same fixed banks at each depth and erase current visited intervals only.
If \(V_j=V_0/W_0^j\), the retained ranges and \(j=O(\log e)\)
give \(\log V_0=O(\log(2V_j))\). Fixed-polynomial shape preparation
therefore fits \(O(V_j)\). Local descriptors list a fixed number of
selected fields and collapsed spectators, not all ancestor subdivisions.
All local temporary padding is removed before return.
This proves Lemma~\ref{lem:swap}.

\section{A closed local E8 routing construction}
\label{app:e8routing}
We combine the folder's
\texttt{integer\_layout\_refinement\_paper/e8\_three\_stage\_kernel.tex}
and \texttt{generic\_routing.tex} at their local finite-network level.
Their conditional outer multiplication conclusions are not premises.
The elementary stream, lower-triangular and local-stack implementations
are those reconstructed in this paper. The new finite data and critical
cost inequality have a separate bounded exact checker.

\subsection{The line system and a restoring scalar word}
Use the 120 rational lines in \(\mathbb Q^8\) represented by
\[
 2e_i\pm2e_j\quad(i<j),\qquad
 (1,\epsilon_2,\ldots,\epsilon_8),
 \quad\epsilon_i\in\{1,-1\},\quad
 \#\{i:\epsilon_i=-1\}\text{ even}.
\]
List the first type by lexicographic pair, minus before plus, and
then list the second by increasing seven-bit negative-sign mask.
Every vector has squared norm eight; distinct inner products are
zero or \(\pm4\). Each line has exactly 63 orthogonal neighbors:
for a first-type line there are 31 first-type and 32 second-type
neighbors, and for a second-type line there are 28 and 35.
These counts follow by overlap and sign counting.

Let \(B_{ss}=1\) and \(B_{st}=[r_s^Tr_t=0]\) for distinct lines.
Over \(\F_2\) there is the explicit factorization
\(B=L^TL\), with nine rows in \(L\). Here is compact data
that fixes it without any numerical rank choice. Use row indices
\[
 (0,1,2,4,6,8,10,12,56)
\]
of \(B\). Their principal Gram rows as nine-bit integers are
\((259,3,260,264,272,288,320,384,509)\), with orthonormal-basis
masks \((1,4,8,16,32,64,131,381,382)\). For column \(s\), form
the nine-bit vector \(b_s\) of the selected \(B\) entries and set
\(L_{a,s}=\operatorname{parity}(e_a\mathbin{\&}b_s)\), using
those masks \(e_a\). The checker verifies all \(120^2\) Gram
entries and the nine-dimensional orthonormality exactly.

Use \(G=L\), \(R=L^T\), nine center roles, and a side role
\((s,t)\) for every ordered distinct orthogonal pair, copying
\(x_t\) into that role and injecting it into \(y_s\).
Then \(RG=B\), \(JV=B+I\), and \(RG+JV=I\) over \(\F_2\).
The eight updates in Lemma~\ref{lem:scalar} therefore implement
\(y\gets y+x\) and restore arbitrary incoming side and center data.
The new probe checks the entire 7809-dimensional local scalar input
space, including scratch, using 64 simultaneous basis columns per pass.
This is finite exact data for the general restoring-word identity.

\subsection{Involutive frames and the central work stream}
For a nondegenerate rational label \(U\) with projector \(P_U\), use
the address permutation
\[
 S_U(H,D)=((I-P_U)H+P_UD,\ P_UH+(I-P_U)D).
\]
It is an involution. For orthogonal summands,
\(S_US_V=S_{U\perp V}\), and \(S_{\mathbb Q^m}\) is the full
exchange of \(H,D\). A comparable-label edge is thus the partial
swap of its orthogonal residual, in either direction.
All labels below lie in the positive definite tensor space
\((\mathbb Q^8)^{\otimes3}\); every required residual is nondegenerate.

Run the three restoring stages forward, inverse with banks exchanged,
and forward, with separate scratch per invocation, on tuples of lines.
Use the eight common labels already displayed in the main proof and
Appendix~\ref{app:labels}, now in this rational geometry. Their
comparability uses only orthogonality of side-neighbor lines.
The logical data endpoint is \((-y,x)\); the sign vanishes on bits.
The routed \(X\)-source has frame \(S_U\), and its \(Y\)-sink
has frame \(S_{U^\perp}\). The other source/sink frames are
\(I,S_{\rm full}\); scratch has those same endpoints.
Consequently every physical input receives \(S_{\rm full}\).
Undo the fixed logical bank permutation by charged split/merge scans.
There is no initial rank-one correction: the first gate already has
the \(X\)-source frame, and \(S_{U^\perp}S_U=S_{\rm full}\).

There is one further local rewrite. A central excursion from frame
\(F_A\) to \(F_B\) and back has involution \(T=F_BF_A^{-1}\)
of residual rank \(h\). For a read-only scatter the original action is
\((c,y)\mapsto(c,y+RTc)\). Keep \(c\), make a disposable copy,
apply \(T\) once to the copy, scatter from it, and erase it.
For a gather of a signed increment \(a\) from unchanged controls,
\(T^{-1}(Tc+a)=c+T^{-1}a\). Form and transform only the increment
once, then add it to \(c\). This is essential in the inverse second
stage, where the center is updated rather than merely read.
Each rewrite removes one of two recursive transforms on that excursion.
Its copies, additions, retained original center and erased work all
cost a fixed number of scans of that role's \(V/W\) bits.
The work stream receives its own fully charged recursive call, still
at volume \(V/W\); it is not an extra productive role or a smaller
fractional-volume call. The other center entry and terminal changes
remain in the invoice.

Put \(v=120,h=8,c=9,z_b=63\), and
\[
 N=v^3=1728000,\quad m=h^3=512,\quad
 W=2N+3v^2(vz_b+c)=330436800,\quad
 L=3v^2ch=3110400.
\]
At a stage with prefix dimension \(p=h^{j-1}\), the complete
rank census after this one-way rewrite is
\[
\begin{array}{c|c}
 \text{residual rank}&\text{multiplicity}\\\hline
 (p-1)(h-1),\ h-1&2N\text{ each}\\
 p-1,\ (p-1)(h-1)+1,\ h-2,\ 1&Nz_b\text{ each}\\
 (p-1)h&cv^2\\
 h&2cv^2\\
 m-ph&Nz_b+cv^2.
\end{array}
\]
Zero ranks are omitted. The first two rows are the data and side
residuals in Appendix~\ref{app:labels}; the next two are the center
entry and two surviving width-\(h\) changes; the last is the full
terminal complement. Sum over \(j=1,2,3\). The rank sum is
\(Wm-2N+L\), with every original role and work call included.
Without the central rewrite it would be \(Wm-2N+2L>Wm\),
so omitting its implementation would destroy the contraction.

\begin{lemma}[A common rational basis for partial-swap pivots]
\label{lem:e8genericpivot}
For any fixed finite family of rational idempotents in dimension \(m\),
one fixed rational basis makes the lower--permutation--lower factor of
every rank-\(r\) partial swap \(S(P)\) have, with \(k=\min(r,m-r)\),
exactly the exchanges
\[
 H_i\leftrightarrow D_{m-1-i}\ (0\le i<k),\qquad
 H_i\leftrightarrow D_i\ (k\le i<r),
\]
and no other exchanges. Its cost is \(r\) narrow interchanges when
\(2r\le m\); otherwise it is \(m-r\) narrow interchanges and
one same-order interchange of width \((2r-m)f\).
The additional triangular work is \(O(V)\), and no external basis
change is applied to the input or output.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write \(R_P(i,j)\) for the rank of the first \(i\) rows and last
\(j\) columns of \(S(P)\), in physical \(H,D\) order. These ranks
are unchanged by invertible lower triangular factors and determine
their permutation factor. For \(r=\operatorname{rank}P\), they
are at most
\[
 U_r(i,j)=\begin{cases}
 \min\{i,j,r,m-r+(i+j-m)_+\},&i,j\le m,\\
 \min\{i,r+(i+j-2m)_+\},&i\le m<j,\\
 \min\{j,r+(i+j-2m)_+\},&j\le m<i,\\
 i+j-2m+U_r(2m-j,2m-i),&i,j>m.
 \end{cases}
\]
The first bound uses both \(P\) and \(I-(I-P)\). For a mixed
corner, add the corresponding \(P\) columns to the added \(I-P\)
columns; only overlapping identity columns remain beyond rank \(r\).
The last bound is the complementary-submatrix rank identity, since
\(S(P)^{-1}=S(P)\).

All bounds are simultaneously attained by an explicit projector.
Put \(\tfrac12\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&1\\1&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\)
on each pair \(\{i,m-1-i\}\), \(0\le i<k\), and put diagonal
ones at the remaining positions \(k\le i<r\). Its rank is \(r\).
Each pair's rank-one factorization from the preceding appendix has
one pivot exchange \(H_i,D_{m-1-i}\); the disjoint diagonal ones
give the stated middle exchanges. Their lower factors have disjoint
supports and combine into lower factors. Counting this permutation
in a first-\(i\)/last-\(j\) corner gives precisely \(U_r(i,j)\).

Every rank-\(r\) rational idempotent is conjugate to this witness.
For each projector and each positive corner bound, select a minor
attaining it at such a conjugate. Its numerator as a function of
\(A\) in \(S(APA^{-1})\) is a nonzero polynomial. Their finite
product, together with \(\det A\), is nonzero over \(\mathbb Q\).
It has a nonzero integer evaluation: if its degree is \(D\), the
grid \(\{0,\ldots,D\}^{m^2}\) suffices by the elementary univariate
root bound and induction. Thus a common integer \(A\) exists and is
found by finite enumeration independent of the input length.

Conjugate all frame labels and the form in the finite description.
All common-frame identities and residual ranks persist, and
\(\operatorname{diag}(A,A)\) commutes with the full swap. The
endpoint is therefore the original physical interchange, without a
runtime \(A\) or \(A^{-1}\) pass. Compile each rational factor
\(L S(P) R=\Pi\) as \(L^{-1}\Pi R^{-1}\). Choose the fixed odd
address prime after these tables, avoiding all denominators and
nonzero pivot/diagonal numerators. Then the same factors hold modulo
every \(q^f\), with unit diagonals. Lower triangular operations and
their inverses are exactly the already priced later-target rotations
and fixed-unit scalings. Only the aligned middle pairs are merged;
the reversed outer pairs stay individually charged.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Independent E8 interchange]
\label{thm:e8swap}
Two equal \(u\)-bit address fields, with arbitrary complete prefix,
gap and suffix fields and arbitrary opaque records, can be exchanged
in \(O(V(u+1)^{\tau_E})\) fixed-tape bit steps, where
\(\tau_E=1-3/2000000\) and \(V\) is their total bit volume.
The construction uses no phase kernel, numerical multiplication
theorem, NTT supplier or number-theoretic hypothesis.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:e8genericpivot} to every residual and work
projector of the finite word just constructed. It changes only their
compilation, not the word or its rank sum. The complete child-width
histogram is
\[
\begin{array}{c|r}
 b&c_b\\\hline
 1&31428518400\\
 370&3456000\\
 372&108864000\\
 384&108993600\\
 496&109123200.
\end{array}
\]
Its first moment is \(\sum_bbc_b=Wm-2N+L\). Each call has the
full current role volume \(V/W\), not that volume divided by its
width. With \(f=\lfloor e/512\rfloor\), the prepared-row cost
divided by current volume satisfies
\[
 F(e)\le\frac1W\sum_bc_bF(bf)+C.
\]
Fewer than 512 leftover radix digits use direct exchanges. Fixed
lower factors, copies, bitwise scalar gates, work streams, row
split/merge, descriptors and local stack movements contribute the
constant parent term. All unselected fields remain complete spectators.

Here is a small exact contraction certificate. Put \(a=3/2000000\),
and for the five widths use the logarithm upper bounds
\[
 \log(512/b)<L_b,\qquad
 (L_1,L_{370},L_{372},L_{384},L_{496})
       ={1\over1000}(6239,326,320,288,32).
\]
Positive 80-term exponential sums already exceed \(512/b\),
proving these bounds. Since \(e^y\le1/(1-y)\) for \(0\le y<1\),
\[
 {1\over W}\sum_bc_b(b/512)^{1-a}
 \le \sum_b\frac{bc_b}{512W(1-aL_b)}
 =\frac{53354609712617889603744709651135881857421875}
        {53354613096691794374204281653199297832914087}
 <1-10^{-8}.
\]
Induction on the actual integer width closes the recurrence at
\(F(e)=O((e+1)^{\tau_E})\), including every finite base width.

The row supply and arbitrary-width wrapper require care. The largest
child ratio is \(496/512=31/32\), and \((31/32)^{32}<1/2\).
Reserve once at the root a budget
\(D_0=32(\lceil\log_2(e+1)\rceil+1)\). Choose the least
\(r\) with \(q^{2r}\ge W^{D_0}\); then \(r=O(\log(e+2))\).
For the fixed small range where \(r\ge e\), use direct digit moves.
Otherwise write the physical fields as
\((H^+,H^-,G,D^+,D^-)\), with the two high parts of width \(r\).
First exchange \(H^+,D^+\), then move \(H^+\) left across
\(H^-,G\). These charged short-field moves give
\((D^+,H^+,H^-,G,D^-)\). Regard the joined high parts' \(q^{2r}\)
values as a row reservoir; the low selected chunks have width \(e-r\).
Pad this row range once to
\(W^{D_0}\lceil q^{2r}/W^{D_0}\rceil<2q^{2r}\).
Every internal call inherits budget \(b-1\) and row count \(R/W\)
from its parent; it neither recomputes a fresh conservative budget
nor adds padding. The root budget bounds every actual descendant depth.

The local LIFO schedule parks inactive current streams, executes one
fully charged child, and returns without scanning parked ancestors.
Both original low-chunk ranges remain present among selected fields
and spectators. As in the earlier interchange proof this gives
\(\log V_0=O(\log(2V_j))\) at depth \(j\), so polynomial
descriptor work is paid by the active child volume. Fixed numbers
of selected controls determine every later-field offset; a large
spectator address is never decoded afresh for each record.
Completed calls restore the entire row index. The core has then changed
the fields to \((D^+,H^+,D^-,G,H^-)\). Only then crop the row pad
and move \(H^+\) right across \(D^-,G\), obtaining precisely
\((D^+,D^-,G,H^+,H^-)\). All high-field movements together cost
\(O(Vr)=O(V\log(e+2))\).
Temporary invalid rows may be nonzero internally; completion restores
them. This smaller toll fits the asserted power.

Finally pad each original binary range numerically to the least
\(q^e\ge2^u\), with factor below \(q^2\) for both fields together.
This is outside the whole radix procedure and is not repeated in a
child. The final full swap preserves the original valid square, so
crop only on its completed boundary. Integer power comparisons select
all widths and budgets. Since \(e=O(u+1)\), the binary bound follows,
uniformly for every record width and spectator range.
\end{proof}

The new \texttt{e8\_generic\_routing\_probe.cpp} independently checks
the E8 geometry and binary factor, all 7809 local scalar basis columns
with arbitrary scratch, the complete rank/child census and both an
outward-log and the displayed coarse rational moment certificate.
It also executes 28 small generic projector factorizations, with
both lower factors, and 80 central-view/increment basis columns.
Controls reject reversed factor words, merging reversed outer pairs,
an omitted view transform and treating a gather as read-only.
Its 999664 assertions run under the self-only 64 MiB/10 CPU-second
limits. They do not execute the huge fixed generic-basis search or
the full E8 role network; those uniform constructions are proved above.

\subsection{Nested boundary blocks give a stronger closed tariff}
We now reconstruct the further coordinate choice from the frozen
\texttt{transposed\_interior\_routing.tex} and
\texttt{e8\_three\_stage\_kernel.tex} notes. The following local proof
uses no outer integer or Gaussian compiler premise.

\begin{lemma}[Partial swaps from projector pivots]
\label{lem:projectorpivots}
Let \(P\) be any rational idempotent. Its northeast matching is found
by reading rows in order, taking each independent row's rightmost
remaining pivot column and eliminating it from later rows. If its
pivots are \((i,j)\), then the permutation factor of \(S(P)\)
consists exactly of the cross exchanges \(H_i,D_j\).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
In the \(H\) rows an independent \(P_i\) has its rightmost pivot
in the \(D\) group, at precisely that column. A dependent row
\(P_i=\sum_{t<i}a_tP_t\), after those eliminations, has left part
\(e_i-\sum_{t<i}a_te_t\); hence its pivot is its own \(H_i\).
This fixes all \(H\) pivots, including \(r=\operatorname{rank}P\)
cross pivots.

For clarity, there cannot be extra exchanges among the remaining
\(D\) indices. Any rational involution is lower-triangularly
conjugate to a signed monomial involution. Here is ordered elimination
proving that assertion. At the least remaining index \(i\), an
isolated row \(\lambda e_i\) has \(\lambda=\pm1\); the lower
first-column operation from \(S^2=I\) isolates its column. Otherwise
let \(j>i\) be the row's last nonzero index, with value \(b\).
Adding column \(j\) to earlier columns by lower conjugation makes
row \(i\) equal \(be_j\), and involution forces row \(j\) to be
\(b^{-1}e_i\). With that pair written conceptually first, let its
remaining columns be \(C_i,C_j\) and remaining block \(A\).
The identity gives \(AC_j=-bC_i\). A lower conjugator with remaining
column-\(i\) increment \(-C_j/b\) removes both columns; all these
remaining indices exceed \(i\), including those below \(j\).
A diagonal scaling makes the pair an ordinary exchange. Continue on
the remaining ordered indices. Each exchange contributes one to
\(\operatorname{rank}(I-S)\), as does each negative fixed point.
Thus the number of exchanges is at most that rank.

Lower factors preserve all northeast corner ranks, whose successive
differences uniquely determine the permutation. Its inverse is the
same permutation because \(S(P)^{-1}=S(P)\). The \(r\) forced
cross exchanges already exhaust
\(\operatorname{rank}(I-S(P))=r\). No additional exchange is
possible. Ordered elimination supplies both rational lower factors;
as before the executed word uses their inverses in the correct order.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[One nested basis for every stage and terminal]
\label{lem:e8nestedprofiles}
There is one fixed rational label basis for the E8 word such that,
at stage prefix \(p>1\), with \(q=p-2\) and \(l=h-2\), the
eight residual types in their displayed census order have child widths
\[
\begin{array}{c|l}
0&1,q,l,ql\\
1&1,l\\
2&1,q\\
3&1,1,q,l,ql\\
4&1,1,h-4\\
5&1\\
6&1,1,q,q,l,ql\\
7&1,1,l.
\end{array}
\]
At \(p=1\), types 0, 2, 6 vanish; the others have respectively
\((1,h-2),(1),(1,1,h-4),(1),(h)\) for types 1, 3, 4, 5, 7.
If the completed prefix has dimension \(d<m\), each terminal
complement has the two widths \(d,m-2d\). Repeated widths are
separate calls. Only increasing consecutive pivot matches are merged.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Choose a ground basis in each tensor factor so every required rank-one
projector has nonzero top-right entry, and every required corank-two
projector has its generic northeast profile. In particular, for
orthogonal ground lines with projectors \(Q,T\), the entries of
\(Q,T,Q+T\) at that corner are nonzero. Each requirement is a
nonempty rational open condition on its fixed-rank projector orbit.
The finite product-of-minors argument in
Lemma~\ref{lem:e8genericpivot} chooses all ground bases at once.

Extend a previous prefix basis of dimension \(p\) by the current
ground basis in dimension \(h\), in current-factor/previous-prefix
order. Keep the first and last \(p\) coordinates fixed, and on the
middle \(lp\) use the fixed coordinate permutation
\[
 e_{ip+j}\longmapsto e_{jl+i},\qquad
 0\le i<l,\quad0\le j<p.
\]
Call this extension \(D\). It conjugates \(X\otimes Y\) to
\(Y\otimes X\) on the middle only. This selects fixed label
coordinates; its full physical effect is included in the new edge's
lower/permutation/lower compilation.

Write \(P\) for the preceding prefix line, \(B=I-P\), and
\(C=I-Q\). The eight projectors, in the stated type order, are
\[
 C\otimes B,\ C\otimes P,\ Q\otimes B,
 \ C\otimes B+T\otimes P,\ (I-Q-T)\otimes P,
 \ Q\otimes P,\ I\otimes B,\ I\otimes P.
\]
Every prefix line retains a nonzero top-right entry: each extension
preserves its two boundary blocks, and that entry is the product of
the old prefix and current ground entries. A rank-one projector with
that entry has one northeast pivot; its complement has one outer
pivot and an aligned middle of length \(p-2\).

For types 0 and 3 the first-to-last block is, respectively,
\(\alpha B\) or \(\alpha B+\beta P\), where
\(\alpha=-Q_{0,h-1}\), \(\beta=T_{0,h-1}\) and
\(\alpha\beta(\alpha-\beta)\ne0\). The first has runs \(1,q\);
the second has runs \(1,q,1\). On the image of \(B\), eliminating
the first outer boundary row leaves the identity in the outer middle:
\[
 C_{ij}-C_{i,h-1}C_{0j}/\alpha=\delta_{ij}
 \quad(1\le i,j<h-1).
\]
The rank-one \(T\) sector of type 3 leaves zero there. Thus in both
cases the middle Schur block is \(I_l\otimes B\), changed by
\(D\) to \(B\otimes I_l\). Its ground outer pivot and aligned
middle give widths \(l,ql\). Boundary and middle ranks exhaust
the whole rank, proving types 0 and 3.

Types 2 and 5 have boundary blocks proportional to \(B,P\) already
exhausting their ranks. For type 1 the boundary is proportional to
\(P\), while the middle becomes \(P\otimes I_l\); these give
\(1,l\). Type 4 uses the two reversed outer pivots of the ground
corank-two projector: one boundary pivot, then in the middle one
outer pivot and a length-\(h-4\) run, all tensored with the single
prefix pivot. This uses the Schur block's rank profile, without
assuming that block is idempotent. For types 6 and 7 there are two
independent boundary copies of \(B,P\); their middles are
\(B\otimes I_l,P\otimes I_l\). This gives the last two rows.
All run widths sum to the actual rank. At \(p=1\) no interior
transpose is needed and the displayed base profiles follow directly.

A later extension multiplies any earlier residual by a future
rank-one projector. Its first-to-last block is a nonzero scalar times
that entire earlier residual and already has its full rank; the
extension preserves those blocks. Hence its entire northeast matching
and every increasing run survive every future extension. For a
terminal prefix projector \(A\) of rank \(d\), the first-\(d\)/
last-\(d\) block is \(\gamma I_d\), \(\gamma\ne0\), also
preserved by future extensions. The complement has that aligned
boundary run of length \(d\); eliminating it leaves the identity
middle of length \(m-2d\). These exhaust its rank \(m-d\).

Lemma~\ref{lem:projectorpivots} turns precisely these matches into
whole partial-swap permutations. All lower factors are fixed and
charged as before. Choose the odd address prime after this new finite
rational family, avoiding its denominators and pivots. Conjugate all
labels and the form by this one nested basis. Pointwise gates and
the full endpoint swap commute with its identical action on the two
banks, so there are no executed endpoint coordinate conversions.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Independent nested E8 interchange]
\label{thm:e8nestedswap}
The interchange in Theorem~\ref{thm:e8swap} has the stronger bit cost
\(O(V(u+1)^{1-301/10^8})\), with all its opaque payload and complete
spectator guarantees, independently of a numerical kernel or multiplier.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Substitute Lemma~\ref{lem:e8nestedprofiles} into the same full rank
census, retaining every terminal and one-way central work call. It gives
\[
\begin{array}{r|r@{\qquad}r|r}
b&c_b&b&c_b\\\hline
1&1760659200&62&221443200\\
4&326592000&64&108993600\\
6&457228800&372&112449600\\
8&109252800&384&108993600\\
36&112449600&496&108993600.
\end{array}
\]
The first moment remains \(Wm-2N+L\). Every call has full role
volume \(V/W\), including calls of larger grouped width. The
root-only row budget and high-field choreography in
Theorem~\ref{thm:e8swap} apply verbatim: \(m=512\) and the maximum
child ratio \(31/32\) are unchanged. No descendant repeats them.
All triangular arithmetic is on original \(f\)-digit coordinates;
a grouped \(bf\)-digit field is used only for pure interchange.

Put \(a=301/10^8\). In increasing width order, take
\[
 (L_b)_b={1\over1000}
 (6239,4853,4447,4159,2655,2112,2080,320,288,32).
\]
For each pair the positive 80-term exponential sum exceeds \(512/b\),
so \(\log(512/b)<L_b\). Direct rational summation gives
\[
 {1\over W}\sum_bc_b(b/512)^{1-a}
 \le\sum_b\frac{bc_b}{512W(1-aL_b)}
 <\frac{999999996}{1000000000}<1.
\]
The separately range-reduced outward logarithm certificate is even
less than \(1-5\cdot10^{-9}\). These are exact rational inequalities
over the displayed finite data. Strong induction therefore closes
the unchanged prepared-row recurrence at \(O(e^{1-a})\), including
direct small widths and remainders. Root overhead \(O(V\log(e+2))\)
is smaller. The one-time binary/radix wrapper, local stacks, fixed
tapes and final crop are the completed operations already proved.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[A limit of rank-preserving E8 packing]
\label{prop:e8packingbarrier}
Keep this E8 word's residual ranks and multiplicities, denominator
\(W\), and full \(V/W\) volume for every recursive call. No
partition of each residual rank into positive child widths can make
this recurrence's normalized moment at exponent \(1-10^{-5}\)
less than one. This is a restriction on that unchanged tariff and
induction, not a lower bound for interchanges or multiplication.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
If widths \(b_i\) sum to rank \(r\), concavity gives
\(\sum_i(b_i/512)^{1-a}\ge(r/512)^{1-a}\), for \(0<a<1\).
Thus merging each entire residual into one width is an optimistic
lower bound, even when no physical layout could realize it. For
\(a=10^{-5}\), use \(e^x\ge1+x\) and lower logarithm bounds to get
\[
 {1\over W}\sum_r n_r(r/512)^{1-a}
 \ge {1\over512W}\sum_r n_rr
                \bigl(1+a\log(512/r)\bigr)
 >\frac{6889615918073066016466949}
         {6889606709951384780800000}>1.
\]
The last rational lower bound uses every residual in the stage census.
It is obtained by the positive 32-term series
\(\log x=2\sum_{j\ge0}t^{2j+1}/(2j+1)\),
\(t=(x-1)/(x+1)\), after reducing integer arguments by powers of
two; the geometric remainder bounds the upper endpoints used in
subtractions. Rounding outwards to multiples of \(2^{-48}\) keeps
the calculation small and rigorous. The finite checker records the
exact lower bound. Hence larger savings also fail this unchanged
moment test. Reaching the public target needs a new word, a deletion
of actual residual calls, or a different fully charged shared schedule.
\end{proof}

The independent \texttt{e8\_nested\_routing\_probe.cpp} checks 69
actual projector profiles in dimensions at most 125, with two equal
and one unequal tensor shapes, including every terminal complement.
It reconstructs both lower factors of 17 entire partial-swap words of
dimension at most 50, including their correctly executed inverse
factors. It rejects 46 whole-rank grouping and 14 wrong-factor controls,
and detects omission of the fixed interior transpose. It independently
sums all ten E8 bins, both exact moment certificates and the scoped
packing obstruction. Its 105679 assertions obey the same self-only
64 MiB/10 CPU-second limits. It builds no 512-dimensional E8 frame or
large role network. The all-size construction is the proof above.
